Issue-Related: 20261107T065214Z-maps-geo-getsprites-sprites-json-index-is-a-synthesized-uniform.md
Issue-Commit: d86ba179c5d8f834b6ff97eae2a4be90662aba63

# maps.geo serves synthesized basemap assets: glyph PBF bytes, sprite raster, sprite index and style descriptor all diverge from the published documents

> _Note: this issue was identified against the code base at commit `d86ba179c5d8f834b6ff97eae2a4be90662aba63`. If you are an issue fixer working on this, the code base you are currently working in can have drifted, so you might need to trace the issue back to the current code base._

## Operation
GetGlyphs, GetSprites, GetStyleDescriptor

## Description
SIX net-new root causes, all in the geo-maps (maps.geo) service, all sharing ONE fault: the three asset-serving operations GetGlyphs, GetSprites and GetStyleDescriptor answer 200 with a document the handler GENERATES at request time from a small stored record, instead of serving the published static AWS asset keyed by the request's URI labels. Every cause below must be closed before this issue is submitted; fixing one asset family leaves the others failing, and fixing one style/colour-scheme leaves the sibling combination failing.

RELATED OPEN ISSUE (does not cover any cause here): 20261107T065214Z-maps-geo-getsprites-sprites-json-index-is-a-synthesized-uniform.md discusses the sprites.json index in prose (Causes 3 and 6 here), but the replay reports owned_by=[] for BOTH of those clusters, so its pointer does not gate them: its Issue-Trace-Ids are bare fixture ids with no '#step' half. The previously-resolved issues 20261023T093543Z / 20261029T182815Z / 20261030T062941Z (GetStyleDescriptor) and 20261102T030925Z (synthesized glyph/sprite assets) are RESOLVED and therefore cover nothing: the same operations still diverge, which is why Causes 1, 2, 4 and 5 are filed net-new. Open issue 20261030T093746Z-geo-maps-four-fidelity-divergences-in-gettile-getstaticmap.md concerns GetTile/GetStaticMap/GetGlyphs REFUSALS and GetTile Content-Type, not these success payloads.

=== Cause 1 of 6: GetGlyphs renders the glyph-range protobuf from font metrics instead of serving the published glyph object ===
AWS: Smithy (services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/smithy-model/com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice.json) binds GetGlyphs to GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; input FontStack (@httpLabel, required, @length max 1000) and FontUnicodeRange (@httpLabel, required, @length max 50, @pattern ^[0-9]+-[0-9]+\.pbf$); output GetGlyphsResponse$Blob as the @httpPayload plus Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag headers; GetGlyphs models NO errors. The model says nothing about the payload's internal bytes, so for the body this is recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 whose body is the published MapLibre/Mapbox glyph-range protobuf for the requested font stack and 256-codepoint range. It is a static published object addressed only by the two URI labels — byte-identical on every call, in every region and every account — so its byte length and its SHA-256 are properties of the asset, not per-run values.
Emulator: 200, but the body is produced at request time by assets::glyph_range_bytes(&font, &input.font_stack, start, end) from whatever metrics the catalog holds for the first resolvable name in the stack (falling back to catalog.fallback_font). The resulting object is a different document: recorded body length 74786 bytes with SHA-256 73b6ca0b7819f2dc763c75c5798d6fcb8527e74105de50744dbe32138c26ac18, emulator body length 139609 bytes with SHA-256 93b5b015200340cdd49f52bc5c9ef34bb8d0054e168311726b3385b3d20ee6b6. HTTP status and header shape match (200 -> 200); the divergence is entirely the payload. The cluster covers four steps across two fixtures, i.e. several font stacks and ranges, so this is the generator and not one bad stored object.
Why: the glyph range carries the SDF bitmaps and the advance/bearing metrics a renderer lays text out with. A synthesized protobuf gives wrong glyph coverage and wrong metrics, so labels render blank, clipped or mis-spaced; and any client that caches or verifies the object by ETag/Content-Length against the published asset gets a mismatch on every fetch.
Fix: the GetGlyphs handler's payload branch plus the glyph asset in this service's state — the stored published glyph-range object must be looked up by (resolved font name, FontUnicodeRange) and returned verbatim, instead of being rendered from font metrics.
Traces: geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#1 (this bundle's representative; the same cluster also covers geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges#task#2, #task#3 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#4). Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_glyphs.rs:181 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 2 of 6: GetSprites sprites.png is rasterized from the emulator's own grid layout instead of the published atlas image ===
AWS: Smithy binds GetSprites to GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; FileName is @httpLabel, required, @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$; Style/ColorScheme/Variant are required @httpLabel enums; output Blob is the @httpPayload with Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag headers; GetSprites models NO errors. Payload bytes are not modelled, so recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 whose body is the published PNG sprite atlas for that (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, pixel ratio) — a static published artwork, identical on every call/region/account, so its length and SHA-256 are asset properties.
Emulator: 200, but the body is drawn at request time: assets::layout_sprites(&sheet, file.pixel_ratio) computes a uniform-grid layout and assets::render_sprite_sheet(&layout) rasterizes it. Recorded body length 121655 bytes with SHA-256 f0d727b230e676e2a204c4f7ec0ab50ff2c73ead3945df40d2532ae0d4746b54; emulator body length 21822 bytes with SHA-256 1b9d66699cbd1b79d450b1ea6f09aaa15543f01eea7ea5ddbcd03e398cd7e180 — roughly one sixth of the published image, i.e. not the published artwork at all. The same cluster covers three steps across two fixtures and two colour variants, so the generated sheet is emitted for every combination.
Why: the raster IS the icon artwork the style's icon-image references resolve into. A renderer handed a generated sheet draws invented shapes (or empty boxes) for every POI marker, one-way arrow and road shield, and a client that verifies or caches the image against the published asset mismatches on every fetch.
Fix: the GetSprites handler's sheet branch plus the sprite asset in this service's state — store the published PNG per (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, FileName including the @2x form) and return it verbatim instead of calling the layout/rasterize path.
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#1 (cluster also covers geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#2 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#2). Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs:204-206 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 3 of 6: GetSprites sprites.json index is a uniform-grid packing, not the published atlas geometry (style/colour-variant fixture) ===
AWS: same GetSprites binding and the same recorded-behavior-only payload contract as Cause 2. Recorded behavior: 200 with a flat JSON object keyed by published icon name, each value carrying that icon's width, height, x and y (and pixelRatio) inside the published sheet. The published packing keeps each icon's natural, non-square footprint and places this whole icon group on the first row (y=0). The geometry is a property of the published asset keyed only by the four URI labels.
Emulator: 200 with an index derived from assets::layout_sprites + assets::sprite_index_json. It forces a few fixed SQUARE footprints onto icons the published index gives non-square ones (recorded 22x20 and 20x20 icons come back 21x21; recorded 5x5 and 6x6 city dots come back 12x12; the *_lowrank dots come back 8x8), it advances x by its own running offset so positions shift by small constants or whole cells, and it WRAPS onto a second row at y=32 for icons the published index keeps at y=0. It also diverges on the icon NAME set: whole-entry divergences are reported for the colon-namespaced $.shield-AE:*, $.shield-AU:*, $.shield-CA:transcanada, $.shield-CN:*, $.shield-CR:*, $.shield-DE:BAB, $.shield-HU:*, $.shield-JP:*, $.shield-KR:*, $.shield-NL:*, $.shield-PH:E, $.shield-PK:*, $.shield-SA:*, $.shield-TR:*, $.shield-TW:*, $.shield-VE:T, $.shield-ch:*, $.shield-cy:* and $.shield-md:* entries, i.e. those names are absent from or renamed in the emulator's index. Status and HTTP shape match (200 -> 200).
Why: this index is the only thing mapping an icon name to a rectangle in the sheet. Wrong width/height crops neighbouring pixels into every symbol; a shifted or second-row x/y addresses atlas coordinates the published sheet never uses, so symbols draw garbage or nothing; and an icon name the index does not carry makes the style's icon-image lookup fail silently.
Fix: the GetSprites handler's sprites.json branch plus the sprite-index asset in this service's state — serve the stored published index document verbatim (names plus per-icon width/height/x/y/pixelRatio) keyed by (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, FileName), instead of computing an atlas layout per request.
Traces: geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants#task#3. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs:204-207 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 4 of 6: GetStyleDescriptor projects a generated MapLibre style document — layer filter expressions, paint, zoom stops and source-layer all diverge (Standard/Dark) ===
AWS: Smithy binds GetStyleDescriptor to GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; Style is the required @httpLabel enum and ColorScheme, PoliticalView, Terrain, ContourDensity, Traffic, TravelModes, Buildings, PoiDensity, PoiCategories and Key are @httpQuery members (color-scheme, political-view, terrain, contour-density, traffic, travel-modes, buildings, poi-density, poi-categories, key); output Blob is the @httpPayload with Content-Type, Cache-Control and ETag headers; GetStyleDescriptor models NO errors. The document's internal structure is not modelled, so recorded behavior only. Recorded behavior: 200 with the PUBLISHED MapLibre style document for that style/colour-scheme/options combination — every layer's source-layer, filter expression, layout and paint members come from the published basemap style release, which is static per combination.
Emulator: 200, but the document is projected from a small stored style record plus the request's knobs by assets::style_descriptor(&catalog, record, &options, request_endpoint(ctx)), so thousands of per-layer members diverge. Representative examples from the recorded diff: layer rail_tunnel's filter is published as terms over ['get','kind'] with value 'transit'/'rail', ['has','is_tunnel'], ['!',['has','service']], a ['case', ...] term and a trailing ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] term — the emulator instead emits 'role'/'way', replaces the ['has','is_tunnel'] term with ['==',['geometry-type'],'LineString'] and DROPS the remaining terms (filter[3], filter[4], filter[5] reported missing); rail_tunnel paint.line-color is published hsl(196, 10%, 18%) and emitted hsl(213, 7%, 25%); rail_tunnel paint.line-width's interpolation stops are published 15 at index 3 and 1.5 at index 6 with further stops 20 and 2, and emitted 12 and 3.0 with indices 7 and 8 missing entirely. The same class of divergence (filter terms, layout, paint, source-layer, minzoom/maxzoom, label layout.text-* members) is reported across the whole layer set — admin_country, admin_country-disputed, admin_country-disputed-mask, admin_label_continent/country/region, admin_region*, airport_label, airport_runway*, bridge_minor*, bridge_motorway-trunk* and onwards.
Why: the style descriptor is the entire client-side rendering contract. A wrong source-layer makes a layer reference a vector-tile layer that does not exist, so nothing draws; a wrong filter draws the wrong features; wrong paint colours and zoom-interpolation stops change colour and line geometry; and a dropped interpolation stop changes the curve above that zoom. A client that renders with this descriptor, or validates it against the published style release, is broken outright.
Fix: the GetStyleDescriptor handler's document branch plus the style asset in this service's state — store the published style document per (Style, ColorScheme) and project ONLY the modelled option knobs onto it, rather than generating layers, filters and paint from a palette/source record.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#1. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:285-290 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state. NOTE: the $.sprite member that also appears in this cluster's diff is NOT part of this cause — it is built from the request's own Host/scheme by request_endpoint (src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:317-356), so it is a per-run host:port value and no requirement.

=== Cause 5 of 6: the same generator serves the second style/colour-scheme, whose published descriptor is a different document ===
AWS: same GetStyleDescriptor binding and the same recorded-behavior-only payload contract, for the other (Style, ColorScheme) combination the rich-options fixture requests. Recorded behavior: that combination's published descriptor is a DIFFERENT document from Cause 4's — it has its own palette (for example rail_tunnel paint.line-color hsl(228, 0%, 91%)) and carries members the other document does not (for example admin_label_continent / admin_label_country / admin_label_region paint.text-halo-color).
Emulator: 200, and emits the same generated layer set with only the palette substituted — so the identical filter/source-layer/zoom-stop divergences recur, plus the members unique to this combination are absent. Because the expected document differs between the two combinations while the generator's output differs only in colour, the replay reports this as its own divergence cluster with its own divergence_key.
Why: same client impact as Cause 4, and it establishes that the descriptor is not the published per-combination asset at all: no client can rely on the descriptor for ANY style. It also means a fix that hardcodes one combination's document leaves this step failing.
Fix: the same GetStyleDescriptor document branch, but the published document must be STORED AND LOOKED UP per (Style, ColorScheme) in this service's state rather than produced from one shared template.
Traces: geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options#task#2. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:267-290 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

=== Cause 6 of 6: the same synthesized sprites.json index is returned for the vector-basemap workflow's style, whose published index has a different icon set ===
AWS: same GetSprites binding and the same recorded-behavior-only payload contract as Cause 3, for the (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) the vector-basemap workflow requests. Recorded behavior: that combination's published index is a DIFFERENT document from Cause 3's — it carries its own icon-name set and its own geometry (for example a dash-and-camel-case $.shield-CA-TransCanada entry with its own width/height/x/y where the other combination publishes the colon-namespaced $.shield-CA:transcanada, and it gives $.truck-parking and $.stadium different footprints).
Emulator: 200 with the IDENTICAL generated uniform-grid index as Cause 3 — same forced 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8 square footprints, same running-x offsets ($.cocktail.x 327 vs 345, $.coffee.x 348 vs 366, $.congestion.x 369 vs 387, $.construction.x 401 vs 419, $.delivery-entrance.x 433 vs 451, $.doctor.x 454 vs 472), same y=32 second-row wrap ($.education 475/y0 vs 0/y32, $.entrance 496/y0 vs 21/y32, $.facilities 516/y0 vs 42/y32, $.fast-food 537/y0 vs 63/y32, $.ferry 558/y0 vs 84/y32, continuing through funicular, game, gas-station, government, grocery, gym, harbour, hospital, information, light-rail, loading-zone, marker*, metro, money, monorail, museum, nature, nightclub, oneway, other, outdoor, park, parking, pharmacy, place-of-worship, police, pub, rail-yard, restaurant, road_closure, salon, shopping, ski, stadium, swimming, taxi, theater, toll-booth, train, truck-parking, truck-services, truck-weigh-station), and the same missing/renamed colon-namespaced shield entries. Because the expected document differs between the two combinations while the emulator's response does not, the replay reports this as its own cluster.
Why: same client impact as Cause 3, and it proves the index is not keyed on the style at all — every (Style, ColorScheme, Variant) gets the same invented atlas. A fix that stores only one combination's index leaves this step failing.
Fix: the same GetSprites sprites.json branch, with the published index stored and looked up PER (Style, ColorScheme, Variant, FileName) in this service's state.
Traces: geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#3. Component: services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_sprites.rs:186-207 and services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/state.

Cause 6 — fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow, step task#3, operation GetSprites, verdict mismatch, 200 / 200, affected_count 1, owned_by=[]. Byte-identical actual index to Cause 3 (same 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8 forced footprints, same running-x offsets, same y=32 wrap, same missing colon-namespaced shields) against a DIFFERENT expected index: this combination's divergence_key carries $.shield-CA-TransCanada.width/.height/.x/.y and gives $.truck-parking.width/.height and $.stadium their own geometry, where Cause 3's expects $.shield-CA:transcanada and different stadium/truck-parking footprints.

EXCLUDED, not filed: cluster 6 of 7 — fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow, step task#1, GetStyleDescriptor, 200 / 200, affected_count 1, owned_by=[], whose ONLY reported key is $.sprite ('<shaped-not-asserted>' vs 'https://127.0.0.1:44519/v2/styles/Standard/Light/Default/sprites/sprites'). That value is built from the request's own Host header / URI scheme by request_endpoint (services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:317-356), so it differs with the listener's host and port in every environment, and the path shape it produces (/v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/sprites, the extension-less MapLibre sprite base that pairs with the modelled FileName @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$) matches AWS. It is a per-run value, not a divergence the emulator caused.

Model evidence (Smithy, services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/smithy-model/com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice.json):
  GetGlyphs -> GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange}, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; FontStack @httpLabel required @length max 1000; FontUnicodeRange @httpLabel required @length max 50 @pattern ^[0-9]+-[0-9]+\.pbf$; output Blob @httpPayload + Content-Type/Cache-Control/ETag; errors: none.
  GetSprites -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/{FileName}, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; FileName @httpLabel required @pattern ^sprites(@2x)?\.(png|json)$; Style (MapStyle), ColorScheme (ColorScheme), Variant (Variant) @httpLabel required enums; output Blob @httpPayload + Content-Type/Cache-Control/ETag; errors: none.
  GetStyleDescriptor -> GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor, code 200 (defaulted), readonly; Style @httpLabel required enum; ColorScheme/PoliticalView/Terrain/ContourDensity/Traffic/TravelModes/Buildings/PoiDensity/PoiCategories/Key as @httpQuery color-scheme/political-view/terrain/contour-density/traffic/travel-modes/buildings/poi-density/poi-categories/key; output Blob @httpPayload + Content-Type/Cache-Control/ETag; errors: none.
  None of the three models the payload document's internal structure, so every payload assertion above is grounded in the recording.

Code evidence (current checkout): services/service-com-amazonaws-geomaps-mapsservice/src/ops/get_glyphs.rs:181 calls assets::glyph_range_bytes(&font, &input.font_stack, start, end); src/ops/get_sprites.rs:204-207 calls assets::layout_sprites(&sheet, file.pixel_ratio) then assets::render_sprite_sheet(&layout) or assets::sprite_index_json(&layout); src/ops/get_style_descriptor.rs:285-290 calls assets::style_descriptor(&catalog, record, &options, request_endpoint(ctx).as_deref()). In all three cases the response body is computed per request from a catalog record rather than read back as a stored published asset.

## Steps to Reproduce
1. git clone the repo (this checkout) and build/boot the emulator from it; the geo-maps service answers on the data listener and /__admin/health returns 200.
2. Replay the simparity-wire-aws-v1 wire corpus (replay_wire_corpus). Result: 4 fixtures, 12/12 steps mismatched, 7 divergence clusters, score_pct 0.0, all service=maps.geo, every cluster stub_path='' and owned_by=[].
3. Cause 1: replay fixture geo-maps-glyphs-fontstack-unicode-ranges step task#1 — GET /v2/glyphs/{FontStack}/{FontUnicodeRange} for the recorded font stack and 256-codepoint range. The emulator answers 200 with a 139609-byte body (sha256 93b5b015…ee6b6) where the recording is 74786 bytes (sha256 73b6ca0b…ac18). Steps task#2, task#3 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#4 fail the same way for other stacks/ranges.
4. Cause 2: replay fixture geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#1 — GET /v2/styles/{Style}/{ColorScheme}/{Variant}/sprites/sprites.png. The emulator answers 200 with a 21822-byte PNG (sha256 1b9d6669…7180); the recorded published atlas is 121655 bytes (sha256 f0d727b2…6b54). Steps task#2 and geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#2 fail the same way for the other colour variants.
5. Cause 3: replay fixture geo-maps-sprites-style-color-variants step task#3 — the sprites.json companion of the same sheet. Diff the returned index against the recorded one: emulator footprints are forced to 21x21 / 12x12 / 8x8 where the published index has 22x20, 20x20, 5x5 and 6x6; x values are shifted by the emulator's running offset; icons the published index keeps at y=0 come back at y=32 with x restarting from 0; and the colon-namespaced $.shield-XX:* entries are absent or renamed.
6. Cause 4: replay fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#1 — GET /v2/styles/{Style}/descriptor with the recorded color-scheme/political-view/terrain/contour-density/traffic/travel-modes/buildings/poi-density/poi-categories query knobs. Diff the returned MapLibre document against the recorded published style: layer filter expressions use different property names and drop trailing terms, paint colours and line-width zoom stops differ, interpolation stops are missing, and source-layer/minzoom/maxzoom and label layout.text-* members differ across the whole layer set. Ignore $.sprite (host-derived).
7. Cause 5: replay fixture geo-maps-style-descriptor-rich-options step task#2 — the same operation for the other (Style, ColorScheme). The emulator emits the same generated layer set with only the palette substituted, so the same divergences recur and members unique to this combination (e.g. admin_label_* paint.text-halo-color) are missing. Fixing only one combination leaves this step failing.
8. Cause 6: replay fixture geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow step task#3 — the sprites.json call for that workflow's style. The emulator returns the byte-identical generated index from Cause 3 while the recorded index for this combination differs (shield-CA-TransCanada with its own geometry, its own truck-parking/stadium footprints), proving the index is not keyed on (Style, ColorScheme, Variant).
9. All six steps named in Issue-Trace/Issue-Step and Issue-Trace-Ids must pass before this issue is closed. Step geo-maps-vector-basemap-style-workflow#task#1 is NOT part of this issue: its only diverging key is the host-derived $.sprite URL.
